Below is a comprehensive training guide and full documentation that captures the recommendations, controls, verifications, and procedures needed to build, operate, and audit an air-gapped, sovereign local AI system. Title: Sovereign Air-Gapped Local AI — Training Guide & Operational Playbook

1 — Purpose and Scope
- Purpose: Provide procedures, verifications, and controls to deploy and operate a truly air-gapped, sovereign local AI system (model hosting, orchestration, data handling, and audit).
- Scope: Applies to model files, inference runtimes, orchestration, storage, network hardening, physical security, logging, and change control for systems that must not communicate with external networks or services.

2 — Core Sovereignty Principles (High-level)
- Zero external dependencies: No cloud APIs, telemetry, or license checks at runtime. Self-host everything.
- Physical and logical isolation: Air-gapped hardware (no internet, Wi‑Fi, Bluetooth, or external network interfaces). Secure boot and BIOS lockdown.
- Static, transparent model behavior: Use open-source, inspectable weights and formats. Freeze model weights; prevent runtime remote updates.
- Local context processing: All context, embeddings, retrievals, and completions must come from local files/repositories and local vector DBs.
- End-to-end auditability: Log all interactions, maintain immutable audit trails, and ensure reproducible builds and signed artifacts.

3 — Recommended Stack Components (local/offline)
- Model hosting: GGUF/GGML-optimized models, llama.cpp, vLLM (compiled locally).
- Orchestration: Self-hosted Prefect, Airflow, or custom Python scripts running locally.
- Data handling: Pandas, SQLite, DuckDB (local only).
- Embedding & search: FAISS, Chroma (offline mode), Weaviate (offline).
- Interface: Gradio, Streamlit, terminal CLI (local-only).
- Security: SELinux/AppArmor, encrypted storage, physical access controls, TPM/HSM for keys.

4 — Environment Preparation (physical & hardware)
- Acquire hardware from trusted vendors where possible.
- Disable or remove unneeded network adapters; ensure no wireless/cellular hardware present when operating in air-gapped mode.
- Configure BIOS/UEFI:
  - Enable Secure Boot (where applicable).
  - Set supervisor password and disable boot from removable media.
  - Disable Wake-on-LAN and remote management (e.g., Intel AMT/ME or AMD PSP features).
- Enable TPM measured/secure boot if available; use measured boot for attestation.
- Limit peripheral usage: no unauthorized removable media; maintain chain-of-custody for any media used.

5 — System Hardening & Isolation
- OS hardening:
  - Use a minimal hardened Linux distribution.
  - Apply SELinux (enforcing) or AppArmor profiles for model processes.
  - Remove unnecessary packages and services (disable network managers).
- Network controls:
  - Physically disconnect network or disable network interfaces.
  - Confirm no routes, no DNS, and no network devices except loopback.
- Container/namespace isolation:
  - Run inference inside containers or unprivileged namespaces with network disabled (e.g., Docker --network=none or Podman equivalent).
  - Restrict filesystem mounts to required paths only; use seccomp and capability dropping.
- Process confinement:
  - Use cgroups to limit resources and capability sets to reduce attack surface.

6 — Artifact Integrity & Reproducible Builds
- Always verify checksums for binaries, models, and containers:
  - Generate SHA256 (or BLAKE2) checksums on an offline verifier machine and store them with the artifact.
  - Verify checksums at installation and at startup.
  - Example:
    - Create: sha256sum model.gguf > model.gguf.sha256
    - Verify: sha256sum -c model.gguf.sha256
- Use GPG to sign important artifacts and Git commits.
- Maintain reproducible build recipes and store them in signed Git repositories.
- Periodically re-verify artifact integrity as part of operations checks.

7 — Model Handling & Safeguards
- Accept only vetted, licensed model artifacts:
  - Keep license text and provenance metadata with each model file.
  - Verify license compatibility for intended usage (commercial vs research).
- Prefer local-only model formats (GGUF/GGML) and runtimes with no remote-check code paths.
- Freeze weights: do not allow runtime weight updates or dynamic downloads.
- Inspect and audit inference code:
  - Search code for network-related calls (e.g., socket, requests, libcurl, curl, http, https, telemetry strings).
  - Remove or sandbox components that allow plugin or external checkpoint loading.
- Hardening inference runtime:
  - Build runtimes from source in the offline reproducible build environment.
  - Strip or audit update-checking code paths.
  - Run model process under least privilege and confinement (AppArmor/SELinux).

8 — Local Context, Embeddings & Vector DBs
- All context and retrieval sources must be local files; maintain strict whitelist of allowed data directories.
- Use FAISS or Chroma in local/offline mode; disable or remove cloud connectors.
- Snapshot vector DB state and store signed checksums for each snapshot.
- Whitelist content sources and prohibit ingestion from removable media unless verified by dedicated offline verifier.

9 — Logging, Audit Trails & Tamper Evidence
- Log everything:
  - Prompts, responses, system actions, config changes, and operator actions.
  - Include timestamps and operator identifiers.
- Append-only logs:
  - Use append-only storage (WORM, write-protected media) or Git with GPG-signed commits for log/event capture.
  - Use tools like AIDE/Tripwire to detect tampering with system files and artifacts.
- Sign critical logs with system keys (HSM/TPM or offline stored key) and maintain offline backup of logs.
- Preserve chain-of-custody for log archives and final audit artifacts.

10 — Practical Verification Commands & Scripts (examples)
- Confirm no network interfaces active:
  - ip link show (expect only loopback)
  - nmcli device status
  - rfkill list all
- Check for active/listening sockets and outbound:
  - ss -tunap
  - lsof -i
- Capture network activity while testing:
  - tcpdump -i any -nn -w capture.pcap
- Verify checksums:
  - sha256sum -c model.gguf.sha256
- Inspect BIOS/firmware:
  - dmidecode -t bios
- Initialize/verify file integrity baseline:
  - AIDE example: aideinit && cp /var/lib/aide/aide.db.new /var/lib/aide/aide.db && aide --check
- Example minimal network-check script (offline verification):
  - #!/bin/bash
  - ip link show | grep -v LOOPBACK || true
  - ss -tunap > /tmp/ss.out
  - if grep -q -E "ESTAB|SYN-SENT|SYN-RECV" /tmp/ss.out; then echo "Unexpected network egress!"; fi
  - (Wrap in an operator-run checklist; do not run on networked systems unless during secure testing.)

11 — Process & Operational Controls
- Change control:
  - Require GPG-signed approvals for model or code updates; verify signatures before deployment.
- Access control:
  - Enforce least privilege for operators; use multi-person authentication for critical operations.
- Key management:
  - Store signing keys in HSM/TPM or offline air-gapped key storage. Use passphrase and physical key custody controls.
- Removable media policy:
  - Disallow or tightly control removable media. If used, always scan, hash, and verify on a dedicated offline verifier machine before ingestion.
- Maintenance windows:
  - Plan offline maintenance with documented procedures, signed approvals, and operator checklists.

12 — Detection, Testing & Red Teaming
- Static analysis:
  - Search dependencies for http, socket, requests, telemetry strings, update, check_version.
  - Use code scanners like Bandit (Python), gosec (Go), shellcheck, and SAST tools locally.
- Dynamic analysis:
  - Exercise all code paths while capturing syscalls (strace), network activity (tcpdump), and file writes.
- Red-team tests:
  - Periodic offline adversarial tests to probe for hidden callbacks, prompt injection, or other unexpected behavior.
- Fuzz/pen-test inputs:
  - Test model with maliciously crafted instructions to detect hallucinated instructions that might attempt to exfiltrate or alter behavior.
- Review random behavior:
  - Validate RNG usage and sources (avoid external entropy sources requiring network access).

13 — Supply-Chain & Firmware Considerations
- Firmware:
  - Keep firmware up to date via reproducible vendor packages and verify signatures.
  - Where possible, audit firmware and microcode; disable unused controllers.
- Hardware procurement:
  - Prefer trusted vendors; insist on chain-of-custody and minimal preinstalled software.
- Peripheral controllers:
  - Disable or remove controllers not required for mission (e.g., microphones, cameras, SD card readers).
- Tamper-evidence:
  - Use tamper-evident seals on enclosures and media. Keep logs of physical access.

14 — Legal & Licensing
- Confirm model license terms and ensure compliance (commercial vs research/distribution restrictions).
- Keep copies of license text with the model artifact and record provenance of model weights.
- If required, consult legal counsel for licensing interpretations before deployment.

15 — Example Minimal Operational Checklist (Daily/Before Use)
- Physically verify network interfaces disabled; ensure no network cables attached.
- Run network check script; ensure no listening or established outbound connections.
- Verify model checksum and signature.
- Start logging and ensure append-only log target is available.
- Start inference container under AppArmor/SELinux profile with network disabled.
- Perform sample queries and verify no network traffic captured.
- Snapshot logs and sign them after the session.

16 — Example Lifecycle: Model Update Procedure (Air-gapped)
- Initiate change request and obtain GPG-signed approvals from required approvers.
- On an isolated staging machine (offline):
  - Verify model checksum and signature.
  - Run static/dynamic scans on model runtime and integration.
  - Generate new artifact checksums and sign them.
- Transport artifact via controlled removable media following chain-of-custody procedures (optional and with strict policy).
- On air-gapped production system:
  - Verify hashes and signatures of incoming artifact against known-good verifier or offline auditor key.
  - Stop services and perform deployment under supervision.
  - Run post-deployment tests and capture signed logs.
  - Record outcome and sign-off.

17 — Immutable Audit Trail & Archive Retention
- Keep signed copies of:
  - Model artifacts, checksums, license text.
  - Build recipes and build artifacts.
  - Signed Git commits for code and config.
  - Append-only logs of prompts/responses and operator actions.
- Use offline archival media (WORM or physically write-protected drives) and maintain multiple copies with chain-of-custody records.
- Retain archives per organizational policy and compliance requirements.

18 — Example AppArmor/SELinux Profile Guidance
- Create a minimal profile that:
  - Disallows network syscalls and socket creation for model process.
  - Restricts file access to allowed directories.
  - Limits capability set (drop NET_ADMIN, SYS_PTRACE, etc.).
- Example Docker run pattern:
  - docker run --rm --network=none --cap-drop=ALL --security-opt seccomp=path/to/seccomp.json --security-opt apparmor:myprofile -v /models:/models:ro my-local-llm
- Ensure the profile is tested and signed as part of reproducible builds.

19 — Scripts & Helpers to Provide (suggested)
- Network verification script (see examples in Section 10).
- Checksum verification script for all artifacts at boot.
- Start/stop service wrapper that ensures AppArmor/SELinux context and logs startup events signed with a system key.
- Log archival and signing tool to append logs to immutable archive.

20 — Monitoring & Periodic Audits
- Periodically re-run:
  - Static dependency scans for newly added packages.
  - File integrity checks (AIDE).
  - Red-team test cases and adversarial prompts.
- Perform scheduled audits of logs, artifact checksums, and signed Git repositories.
- Conduct at least annual supply-chain and firmware audits.

21 — Practical Pitfalls & Common Mistakes
- Overlooking telemetry baked into third-party libraries.
- Failing to disable or physically remove wireless hardware.
- Accepting models without checking licensing or provenance.
- Allowing dynamic plugin loading or untrusted code paths in runtimes.
- Using network-enabled container orchestration without disabling egress.
- Neglecting signed, reproducible builds or failing to store checksums offline.
- Not enforcing strict chain-of-custody for removable media or hardware maintenance.

22 — Emergency & Incident Response (air-gapped)
- If unexpected network activity detected:
  - Immediately power down the system in a controlled manner.
  - Preserve memory and disk images if required by investigation policies.
  - Notify chain-of-command and incident response team.
  - Use offline forensic workstation to analyze logs, pcap, and filesystem snapshots.
- If model compromise suspected:
  - Quarantine affected artifacts and revert to last known-good signed artifact.
  - Re-audit toolchain and runtime.
  - Rebuild from reproducible build recipes if necessary.

2) Network verification script (save as verify_no_network.sh, make executable)

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
OUT=/tmp/network_check.$(date +%s).txt

echo "Network interfaces:" > "$OUT"
ip link show >> "$OUT" 2>&1
echo -e "\nActive routes:" >> "$OUT"
ip route show >> "$OUT" 2>&1
echo -e "\nSockets:" >> "$OUT"
ss -tunap >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true
echo -e "\nLsof network:" >> "$OUT"
lsof -i >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true

# Check for non-loopback UP interfaces
if ip -o link show up | grep -v " lo:" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "ERROR: Non-loopback interface is UP" >&2
  cat "$OUT"
  exit 2
fi

# Check for established/outbound sockets
if ss -tunap | grep -E "ESTAB|SYN-SENT|SYN-RECV" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "ERROR: Unexpected network connections detected" >&2
  cat "$OUT"
  exit 3
fi

echo "No unexpected network interfaces or connections found."
exit 0


3) Checksum & signature verification script (save as verify_artifacts.sh)

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# Configure these paths
MODEL=/models/model.gguf
MODEL_SHA=/models/model.gguf.sha256
MODEL_SIG=/models/model.gguf.sig
BINARY=/usr/local/bin/llm_runtime
BINARY_SHA=/etc/llm/binary.sha256
GPG_KEY_ID="your-audit-key-id"

fail() { echo "VERIFICATION FAILED: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

# Check files exist
[ -f "$MODEL" ] || fail "Model not found: $MODEL"
[ -f "$MODEL_SHA" ] || fail "Model sha file not found: $MODEL_SHA"
[ -f "$BINARY" ] || fail "Runtime binary not found: $BINARY"
[ -f "$BINARY_SHA" ] || fail "Binary sha file not found: $BINARY_SHA"

# Checksum verification
sha256sum -c "$MODEL_SHA" || fail "Model checksum mismatch"
sha256sum -c "$BINARY_SHA" || fail "Binary checksum mismatch"

# GPG signature verify (model)
gpg --verify "$MODEL_SIG" "$MODEL" || fail "GPG signature verification failed for model"

echo "All artifact checks passed."
exit 0


4) Minimal service wrapper (save as model_service.sh, make executable). Assumes local runtime binary or container, logging to append-only directory.

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

SERVICE_NAME="local-llm"
LOG_DIR="/var/log/llm_append_only"
PIDFILE="/var/run/${SERVICE_NAME}.pid"
MODEL_PATH="/models/model.gguf"
GPG_SIGN_KEY="system-sign-key"

timestamp(){ date -u +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"; }

start(){
  echo "$(timestamp) Starting ${SERVICE_NAME}" | tee -a "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
  # Verify artifacts before start
  /opt/verify_artifacts.sh || { echo "Artifact verification failed"; exit 1; }
  # Start the runtime (example: non-network container)
  docker run --rm --name "${SERVICE_NAME}" \
    --network=none --cap-drop=ALL \
    --security-opt apparmor:llm_profile \
    -v /models:/models:ro -v "${LOG_DIR}":/logs \
    my-llm:local > "${LOG_DIR}/stdout.log" 2> "${LOG_DIR}/stderr.log" &
  echo $! > "$PIDFILE"
  echo "$(timestamp) START ${SERVICE_NAME} PID=$(cat $PIDFILE)" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
  # Sign start event
  echo "$(timestamp) START ${SERVICE_NAME} PID=$(cat $PIDFILE)" | gpg --clear-sign -u "$GPG_SIGN_KEY" > "${LOG_DIR}/last_start.sig"
}

stop(){
  if [ -f "$PIDFILE" ]; then
    PID=$(cat "$PIDFILE")
    echo "$(timestamp) Stopping ${SERVICE_NAME} PID=$PID" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
    kill "$PID" || true
    rm -f "$PIDFILE"
    echo "$(timestamp) STOP ${SERVICE_NAME}" | gpg --clear-sign -u "$GPG_SIGN_KEY" > "${LOG_DIR}/last_stop.sig"
  else
    echo "Service not running (no pidfile)." >&2
  fi
}

status(){
  if [ -f "$PIDFILE" ]; then
    echo "Running, PID=$(cat $PIDFILE)"
  else
    echo "Stopped"
  fi
}

case "${1:-}" in
  start) start ;;
  stop) stop ;;
  status) status ;;
  *) echo "Usage: $0 {start|stop|status}" ; exit 2 ;;
esac


Notes:
- Ensure ${LOG_DIR} is on append-only/WORM media or write-protected filesystem; restrict permissions (root-only).
- Replace my-llm:local with your local-built image name or runtime invocation. If using a binary instead of container, start the binary under AppArmor/SELinux confinement and redirect stdout/stderr to the log directory.

5) Example AppArmor profile (save as /etc/apparmor.d/usr.local.bin.llm then apparmor_parser -r /etc/apparmor.d/usr.local.bin.llm)

# AppArmor profile: llm_profile
profile llm_profile flags=(attach_disconnected,mediate_deleted) {
  # Deny network
  network,  # deny all network family permissions

  # Allow read-only access to models
  /models/** r,
  /etc/ssl/certs/** r,
  /usr/lib/** r,
  /lib/** r,
  /lib64/** r,

  # Logs: allow appending to log directory only
  /var/log/llm_append_only/** wkl,  # write, append, lock

  # Deny execution of user-supplied plugins
  deny /models/** mrwklx,

  # Basic file access for runtime
  /usr/local/bin/llm_runtime rix,
  /tmp/** rw,
  capability net_raw drop,
  capability net_admin drop,
  capability sys_ptrace drop,
  deny @{PROC}/** rwklx,

  # Deny ptrace and setuid exec
  deny /bin/su ix,
  deny /usr/bin/sudo ix,

  # Allow reading system libs and locales
  /usr/share/locale/** r,
  /etc/localtime r,

  # Deny any ptrace/open of other processes
  deny /proc/*/mem r,
}

Notes:
- AppArmor syntax varies; test profile in complain mode first, then enforce.
- The network, token denies networking — confirm syntax per AppArmor version; you may need to explicitly deny socket/create calls or use seccomp for syscall-level blocks.

6) Example Dockerfile + run command
Dockerfile (build locally in air-gapped build environment)

dockerfile
FROM ubuntu:22.04
RUN apt-get update && apt-get install -y --no-install-recommends \
    ca-certificates libstdc++6 libgomp1 python3 python3-pip && \
    rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*

# Add local runtime binary built offline
COPY llm_runtime /usr/local/bin/llm_runtime
RUN chmod 755 /usr/local/bin/llm_runtime

# Set minimal user
RUN groupadd -r llm && useradd -r -g llm llm
USER llm
WORKDIR /home/llm

ENTRYPOINT ["/usr/local/bin/llm_runtime", "--model", "/models/model.gguf", "--log-dir", "/logs"]


Build (on offline build host):

docker build -t my-llm:local .


Run on production air-gapped host:

# Ensure AppArmor profile 'llm_profile' loaded and LOG_DIR exists (append-only)
mkdir -p /var/log/llm_append_only
chown root:root /var/log/llm_append_only
chmod 700 /var/log/llm_append_only

docker run --rm --name local-llm \
  --network=none \
  --cap-drop=ALL \
  --security-opt apparmor:llm_profile \
  --security-opt no-new-privileges \
  -v /models:/models:ro \
  -v /var/log/llm_append_only:/logs:rw \
  my-llm:local

1) AppArmor enforcement profile (finalized)

text
# /etc/apparmor.d/usr.local.bin.llm_profile
profile llm_profile flags=(attach_disconnected,mediate_deleted) {
  # Deny network operations
  deny network,

  # Allow execution of runtime binary
  /usr/local/bin/llm_runtime ixr,

  # Read-only access to model files and libs
  /models/** r,
  /usr/lib/** r,
  /lib/** r,
  /lib64/** r,
  /usr/share/locale/** r,
  /etc/localtime r,

  # Append-only logging directory (restricted)
  /var/log/llm_append_only/** wkl,

  # Temporary file usage limited
  /tmp/** rw,

  # Deny execution of any files from /models
  deny /models/** mrwklx,

  # Prevent reading/writing proc memory of other processes
  deny /proc/*/mem r,

  # Capability drops
  capability net_raw drop,
  capability net_admin drop,
  capability sys_ptrace drop,

  # Deny mounting and remounting
  deny mount,

  # Deny ptrace via /proc
  deny /proc/*/task/*/mem r,

  # Allow basic signals for process control
  signal (receive) peer=unconfined,

  # Common read access
  /etc/ssl/certs/** r,
}


Notes: test in complain mode (aa-complain /etc/apparmor.d/usr.local.bin.llm_profile) first, adjust paths and allowed libs, then enforce with aa-enforce.

2) seccomp JSON (restrict syscalls)

json
{
  "defaultAction": "SCMP_ACT_ERRNO",
  "archMap": [
    { "architecture": "SCMP_ARCH_X86_64", "subArchitectures": ["SCMP_ARCH_X86"] }
  ],
  "syscalls": [
    { "names": ["read", "write", "exit", "exit_group", "rt_sigreturn", "rt_sigaction", "sigaltstack"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ALLOW" },
    { "names": ["open", "openat", "close", "fstat", "lseek", "mmap", "mprotect", "munmap", "brk"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ALLOW" },
    { "names": ["arch_prctl", "set_tid_address", "futex", "clock_gettime", "getrandom"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ALLOW" },
    { "names": ["stat", "lstat", "access", "dup", "dup2", "pread64", "pwrite64"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ALLOW" },
    { "names": ["clone", "fork", "vfork"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ALLOW" },
    { "names": ["execve"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ALLOW" },
    { "names": ["socket", "connect", "accept", "bind", "listen", "sendto", "recvfrom", "sendmsg", "recvmsg"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ERRNO" },
    { "names": ["ptrace", "kcmp", "process_vm_readv", "process_vm_writev"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ERRNO" },
    { "names": ["setns", "unshare", "mount", "umount2"], "action": "SCMP_ACT_ERRNO" }
  ]
}

Notes: adapt allowed syscalls to your runtime (some runtimes need specific syscalls such as futex and getrandom). Validate with docker run --security-opt seccomp=/path/seccomp.json ... and adjust as needed.

3) systemd unit file (wraps model_service.sh)

ini
# /etc/systemd/system/local-llm.service
[Unit]
Description=Local Air-Gapped LLM Service
After=local-fs.target
Requires=network-pre.target
Documentation=man:systemd.unit

[Service]
Type=simple
User=llm
Group=llm
ExecStart=/opt/model_service.sh start
ExecStop=/opt/model_service.sh stop
ExecReload=/opt/model_service.sh restart
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5s
Environment=LOG_DIR=/var/log/llm_append_only
# Prevent service from gaining new privileges
NoNewPrivileges=true
ProtectSystem=full
ProtectHome=true
PrivateTmp=true
ProtectKernelTunables=true
ProtectKernelModules=true
ProtectControlGroups=true
RestrictAddressFamilies=~AF_INET AF_INET6 AF_NETLINK
SystemCallFilter=@default @clock @cpu-emulation @file @keyring @memory @network-io
RuntimeDirectory=local-llm
RuntimeDirectoryMode=0750

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target


Notes:
- SystemCallFilter groups require systemd with syscall group support; you can replace with specific SystemCallFilter entries or use seccomp via container runtime.
- Ensure model_service.sh is owned by root and executable; the runtime binary should be owned by a non-root user (llm).

SOVEREIGN AIR-GAPPED AI — DAILY CHECKLIST
Date: __________  Operator: __________

Shutdown

Post-session

Emergency

2) AppArmor enforcement profile (save as /etc/apparmor.d/usr.local.bin.llm_profile)

# AppArmor profile: llm_profile
profile llm_profile flags=(attach_disconnected,mediate_deleted) {
  # Deny networking
  deny network,

  # Allow execution of runtime binary
  /usr/local/bin/llm_runtime ixr,

  # Read-only access to models and libs
  /models/** r,
  /usr/lib/** r,
  /lib/** r,
  /lib64/** r,
  /usr/share/locale/** r,
  /etc/localtime r,
  /etc/ssl/certs/** r,

  # Append-only logging directory (restricted)
  /var/log/llm_append_only/** wkl,

  # Temporary file usage limited
  /tmp/** rw,

  # Deny execution of files under /models
  deny /models/** mrwklx,

  # Prevent reading/writing proc memory of other processes
  deny /proc/*/mem r,
  deny /proc/*/task/*/mem r,

  # Capability drops
  capability net_raw drop,
  capability net_admin drop,
  capability sys_ptrace drop,

  # Deny mount operations
  deny mount,

  # Allow basic signals
  signal (receive) peer=unconfined,
}

Notes: test with aa-complain /etc/apparmor.d/usr.local.bin.llm_profile first, adjust allowed libs, then aa-enforce.

Optional helper scripts (network check + artifact verify + service wrapper). Save them and mark executable:

bash
cat > verify_no_network.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
OUT=/tmp/network_check.$(date +%s).txt
ip link show > "$OUT" 2>&1
ip route show >> "$OUT" 2>&1
ss -tunap >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true
lsof -i >> "$OUT" 2>&1 || true
if ip -o link show up | grep -v " lo:" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "ERROR: Non-loopback interface is UP" >&2
  cat "$OUT"
  exit 2
fi
if ss -tunap | grep -E "ESTAB|SYN-SENT|SYN-RECV" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "ERROR: Unexpected network connections detected" >&2
  cat "$OUT"
  exit 3
fi
echo "No unexpected network interfaces or connections found."
exit 0
EOF

cat > verify_artifacts.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
MODEL=/models/model.gguf
MODEL_SHA=/models/model.gguf.sha256
MODEL_SIG=/models/model.gguf.sig
BINARY=/usr/local/bin/llm_runtime
BINARY_SHA=/etc/llm/binary.sha256
fail() { echo "VERIFICATION FAILED: $*" >&2; exit 1; }
[ -f "$MODEL" ] || fail "Model not found: $MODEL"
[ -f "$MODEL_SHA" ] || fail "Model sha file not found: $MODEL_SHA"
[ -f "$BINARY" ] || fail "Runtime binary not found: $BINARY"
[ -f "$BINARY_SHA" ] || fail "Binary sha file not found: $BINARY_SHA"
sha256sum -c "$MODEL_SHA" || fail "Model checksum mismatch"
sha256sum -c "$BINARY_SHA" || fail "Binary checksum mismatch"
gpg --verify "$MODEL_SIG" "$MODEL" || fail "GPG signature verification failed for model"
echo "All artifact checks passed."
exit 0
EOF

cat > model_service.sh <<'EOF'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail
SERVICE_NAME="local-llm"
LOG_DIR="/var/log/llm_append_only"
PIDFILE="/var/run/${SERVICE_NAME}.pid"
timestamp(){ date -u +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ"; }
start(){
  echo "$(timestamp) Starting ${SERVICE_NAME}" | tee -a "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
  /opt/verify_artifacts.sh || { echo "Artifact verification failed"; exit 1; }
  docker run --rm --name "${SERVICE_NAME}" --network=none --cap-drop=ALL --security-opt apparmor:llm_profile -v /models:/models:ro -v "${LOG_DIR}":/logs my-llm:local > "${LOG_DIR}/stdout.log" 2> "${LOG_DIR}/stderr.log" &
  echo $! > "$PIDFILE"
  echo "$(timestamp) START ${SERVICE_NAME} PID=$(cat $PIDFILE)" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
}
stop(){
  if [ -f "$PIDFILE" ]; then
    PID=$(cat "$PIDFILE")
    echo "$(timestamp) Stopping ${SERVICE_NAME} PID=$PID" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
    kill "$PID" || true
    rm -f "$PIDFILE"
    echo "$(timestamp) STOP ${SERVICE_NAME}" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
  else
    echo "Service not running (no pidfile)." >&2
  fi
}
status(){
  if [ -f "$PIDFILE" ]; then
    echo "Running, PID=$(cat $PIDFILE)"
  else
    echo "Stopped"
  fi
}
case "${1:-}" in
  start) start ;;
  stop) stop ;;
  status) status ;;
  *) echo "Usage: $0 {start|stop|status}" ; exit 2 ;;
esac
EOF

chmod +x verify_no_network.sh verify_artifacts.sh model_service.sh

A — systemd unit (save as /etc/systemd/system/local-llm.service)

[Unit]
Description=Local Air-Gapped LLM Service
After=local-fs.target
Requires=local-fs.target

[Service]
Type=simple
User=llm
Group=llm
# Ensure seccomp file is in place (copy from /etc/llm/seccomp.json to runtime-accessible path)
ExecStartPre=/bin/mkdir -p /run/local-llm
ExecStartPre=/bin/cp -a /etc/llm/seccomp.json /run/local-llm/seccomp.json
# Start via service wrapper; wrapper must not attempt network operations
ExecStart=/opt/model_service.sh start
ExecStop=/opt/model_service.sh stop
ExecReload=/opt/model_service.sh restart
Restart=on-failure
RestartSec=5s
Environment=LOG_DIR=/var/log/llm_append_only
NoNewPrivileges=true
ProtectSystem=full
ProtectHome=true
PrivateTmp=true
ProtectKernelTunables=true
ProtectKernelModules=true
ProtectControlGroups=true
RestrictAddressFamilies=~AF_INET AF_INET6 AF_NETLINK
RuntimeDirectory=local-llm
RuntimeDirectoryMode=0750
# If systemd supports seccomp via systemd Service SeccompProfile=, use it; otherwise runtime/container should mount /run/local-llm/seccomp.json
#SeccompProfile=/run/local-llm/seccomp.json

[Install]
WantedBy=multi-user.target

Notes:
- If your systemd supports SeccompProfile=, set it to /run/local-llm/seccomp.json. Otherwise ensure container runtime is invoked with --security-opt seccomp=/run/local-llm/seccomp.json.
- Ensure /opt/model_service.sh is owned root:root 700 and does not perform network actions. Ensure /etc/llm/seccomp.json exists and is audited.

B — Burn/package ZIP+PDF to write-protected media (recommended workflow)

1) Prepare archive and checksum (offline machine)

bash
# from working dir (e.g., ~/airgapped_llm_pkg)
zip -r airgapped_llm_pkg.zip ./*
sha256sum airgapped_llm_pkg.zip > airgapped_llm_pkg.zip.sha256


2) Create a signed manifest (optional) — sign locally if you have a key

bash
# Plaintext manifest
cat > manifest.txt <<'EOF'
airgapped_llm_pkg.zip
airgapped_llm_pkg.zip.sha256
airgapped_llm_reference.pdf
EOF

# (Optional) detached signature if you have a signing key locally:
gpg --detach-sign --armor --output manifest.txt.asc manifest.txt


3) Burn to read-only media (USB with write-protect switch or optical)
- USB (if hardware write-protect switch present):
  - Copy files to USB on offline machine:
    
bash
    mkdir /mnt/usb && mount /dev/sdX1 /mnt/usb
    cp airgapped_llm_pkg.zip airgapped_llm_pkg.zip.sha256 airgapped_llm_reference.pdf manifest.txt /mnt/usb/
    sync; umount /mnt/usb

  - Physically set USB write-protect switch (if available) and retain chain-of-custody seals.
- Optical (recommended when permanent WORM desired):
  - Create ISO and burn:
    
bash
    mkdir iso_root && cp airgapped_llm_pkg.zip airgapped_llm_pkg.zip.sha256 airgapped_llm_reference.pdf manifest.txt iso_root/
    genisoimage -o airgapped_llm_pkg.iso -V AIRGAP_$(date +%Y%m%d) -r iso_root/
    wodim dev=/dev/cdrw -v -data airgapped_llm_pkg.iso

  - Label disc, seal, and store in secure location.

4) Verify media integrity on retrieval (every time)
- Always verify checksum before use:

bash
sha256sum -c airgapped_llm_pkg.zip.sha256
# If manifest signed:
gpg --verify manifest.txt.asc manifest.txt

- Confirm PDF opens and templates match expected content.

5) Chain-of-custody & physical controls
- Log: who created archive, who burned media, date/time, device serials.
- Seal media with tamper-evident labels; record seal IDs in log.

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

LOG_DIR=/var/log/llm_append_only
SECCOMP=/run/local-llm/seccomp.json
IMAGE=my-llm:local
CONTAINER_NAME=local-llm
PIDFILE=/var/run/${CONTAINER_NAME}.pid

mkdir -p "$LOG_DIR"
chown root:root "$LOG_DIR"
chmod 700 "$LOG_DIR"

[ -f "$SECCOMP" ] || { echo "Missing seccomp file: $SECCOMP" >&2; exit 1; }

# Ensure container not already running
if [ -f "$PIDFILE" ] && kill -0 "$(cat "$PIDFILE")" 2>/dev/null; then
  echo "Container appears to be running (PID $(cat "$PIDFILE"))." >&2
  exit 1
fi

docker run --rm --name "$CONTAINER_NAME" \
  --network=none \
  --cap-drop=ALL \
  --security-opt apparmor:llm_profile \
  --security-opt seccomp="$SECCOMP" \
  --security-opt no-new-privileges \
  --read-only \
  --tmpfs /tmp:rw,mode=1777 \
  -v /models:/models:ro \
  -v "$LOG_DIR":/logs:rw \
  "$IMAGE" > "$LOG_DIR/stdout.log" 2> "$LOG_DIR/stderr.log" &

echo $! > "$PIDFILE"
echo "Started $CONTAINER_NAME PID=$(cat "$PIDFILE")"


2) Short operator checklist (save as /opt/llm_operator_checklist.txt, chmod 600)

AIR-GAPPED LLM OPERATOR CHECKLIST — START / STOP

Prepare (before start)
- Confirm media, model, binaries verified (sha256 & GPG) on this host.
- Confirm /etc/llm/seccomp.json present and /run/local-llm/seccomp.json populated (systemd ExecStartPre copies it).
- Confirm AppArmor profile 'llm_profile' loaded and in enforce mode (or tested in complain mode).
- Ensure /var/log/llm_append_only exists, root-owned, perms 700.
- Confirm Docker image 'my-llm:local' available locally.
- Confirm no network interfaces up (run /opt/verify_no_network.sh).

Start procedure
1. Run artifact verification: /opt/verify_artifacts.sh  -> must exit 0
2. Start service via systemd: systemctl start local-llm.service
   OR run wrapper (if using script): /opt/run_llm_container.sh
3. Confirm PID file exists: cat /var/run/local-llm.pid
4. Tail logs: tail -f /var/log/llm_append_only/stdout.log /var/log/llm_append_only/stderr.log
5. Run a benign sample query; record prompt + response in append-only log.

Operational monitoring
- Periodically run: /opt/verify_no_network.sh
- Check for unexpected outgoing connections: ss -tunap | grep -E 'ESTAB|SYN-SENT|SYN-RECV'
- Watch filesystem for unexpected writes (inotify/auditd).

Shutdown procedure
1. Stop via systemd: systemctl stop local-llm.service
   OR kill via PID in /var/run/local-llm.pid and wait for container removal.
2. Verify container stopped: [ ! -f /var/run/local-llm.pid ] and docker ps | grep local-llm
3. Sign & archive session logs to offline WORM media. Record checksums.

Post-session verification
- Run /opt/verify_no_network.sh
- Verify checksums of model & runtime again.
- Update chain-of-custody log: operator name, times, media IDs, checksums.

Emergency (if suspicious activity)
- Immediately power down host preserving storage media.
- Do not reboot. Notify Incident Response and follow chain-of-custody.


If you want, I can also provide a short systemd ExecStop helper to ensure the container is cleanly killed and PID file removed.

Below is a minimal, safe ExecStop helper and a matching systemd-friendly stop script.

1) Save as /opt/stop_llm_container.sh, then chown root:root /opt/stop_llm_container.sh && chmod 700 /opt/stop_llm_container.sh

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

CONTAINER_NAME=local-llm
PIDFILE=/var/run/${CONTAINER_NAME}.pid
LOG_DIR=/var/log/llm_append_only
TIMEOUT=15

if [ -f "$PIDFILE" ]; then
  PID=$(cat "$PIDFILE")
  if kill -0 "$PID" 2>/dev/null; then
    echo "Stopping container wrapper PID=$PID" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
    kill "$PID" || true
    # wait for process exit
    for i in $(seq 1 $TIMEOUT); do
      if ! kill -0 "$PID" 2>/dev/null; then
        break
      fi
      sleep 1
    done
  fi
  rm -f "$PIDFILE"
fi

# Ensure docker container removed (best-effort)
if docker ps -a --format '{{.Names}}' | grep -q "^${CONTAINER_NAME}$"; then
  echo "Stopping docker container ${CONTAINER_NAME}" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"
  docker stop --time=10 "${CONTAINER_NAME}" || true
  docker rm -f "${CONTAINER_NAME}" || true
fi

echo "$(date -u +"%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ") STOP ${CONTAINER_NAME}" >> "${LOG_DIR}/service.log"


2) To integrate with systemd unit, add or update ExecStop to call this script (example fragment)

ExecStop=/opt/stop_llm_container.sh
KillMode=control-group
TimeoutStopSec=30


That's it — save the script, set ownership and permissions, and reload systemd if you updated the unit: systemctl daemon-reload.

1) /opt/verify_artifacts.sh

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# Usage: /opt/verify_artifacts.sh /path/to/artifacts_dir
ARTDIR="${1:-.}"
MANIFEST="${ARTDIR}/manifest.txt"
SHAFILE="${ARTDIR}/airgapped_llm_pkg.zip.sha256"

# Verify manifest signature if present
if [ -f "${MANIFEST}.asc" ]; then
  gpg --verify "${MANIFEST}.asc" "${MANIFEST}" || { echo "Manifest GPG verification FAILED"; exit 2; }
fi

# Verify SHA256 checksums (expects file name + checksum)
if [ -f "${SHAFILE}" ]; then
  (cd "${ARTDIR}" && sha256sum -c "$(basename "$SHAFILE")") || { echo "SHA256 verification FAILED"; exit 3; }
else
  echo "Missing checksum file: ${SHAFILE}" >&2
  exit 4
fi

# Optional: verify expected individual artifact checksums stored centrally
if [ -f /etc/llm/expected_hashes.sha256 ]; then
  (cd "${ARTDIR}" && sha256sum -c /etc/llm/expected_hashes.sha256) || { echo "Expected artifacts mismatch"; exit 5; }
fi

echo "OK"
exit 0


2) /opt/verify_no_network.sh

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# Ensure no interfaces other than loopback are UP with addresses
ip -4 addr show scope global | grep -q . && {
  echo "Network interfaces with IPv4 addresses present:" >&2
  ip -4 addr show scope global
  exit 1
}

# Check for established or outgoing TCP/UDP connections
if ss -tunap | grep -E 'ESTAB|SYN-SENT|SYN-RECV' >/dev/null; then
  echo "Active network connections detected:" >&2
  ss -tunap | grep -E 'ESTAB|SYN-SENT|SYN-RECV'
  exit 2
fi

# Ensure no default route (optional)
if ip route show default >/dev/null 2>&1 && ip route show default | grep -q default; then
  echo "Default route present:" >&2
  ip route show default
  exit 3
fi

echo "NO_NETWORK_OK"
exit 0


3) /opt/verify_start_preconditions.sh

bash
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

# Basic pre-start checks: files, seccomp, apparmor, docker image
[ -f /run/local-llm/seccomp.json ] || { echo "Missing /run/local-llm/seccomp.json" >&2; exit 1; }
if command -v aa-status >/dev/null; then
  aa-status --enabled >/dev/null 2>&1 || echo "AppArmor not enabled / active"
fi

# Docker image presence
IMAGE="my-llm:local"
if ! docker image inspect "${IMAGE}" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
  echo "Docker image ${IMAGE} not present" >&2
  exit 2
fi

# Log dir perms
[ -d /var/log/llm_append_only ] || mkdir -p /var/log/llm_append_only
stat -c "%U %G %a" /var/log/llm_append_only

# Run artifact & network checks
/opt/verify_artifacts.sh /opt/artifacts || exit 3
/opt/verify_no_network.sh || exit 4

echo "PRECONDITIONS_OK"
exit 0

⸻

🧮 What is the Mandelbrot Set?

At its core, the Mandelbrot Set is a fractal — a mathematical set that repeats its pattern at every scale. That means infinite complexity from a simple rule.

📐 The Core Equation:

z_{n+1} = z_n^2 + c
	•	z is a complex number (which includes both real and imaginary parts)
	•	c is a constant (also a complex number)
	•	You start with z_0 = 0, and iterate infinitely.
	•	The question is: Does the value of z_n stay bounded (doesn’t escape to infinity)?

If it stays bounded, c is in the Mandelbrot Set.
If it escapes to infinity, c is not in the set.

⸻

🌀 Why Is This Powerful?

Because this simple formula creates infinitely detailed, self-repeating visual structures. Every time you zoom in, new variations emerge — never-ending landscapes, coastlines, lightning bolts, spirals… yet all from that one rule.

⸻

🎮 Use in Video Games: “No Man’s Sky” Example

In No Man’s Sky, the Mandelbrot-like fractal math is used to procedurally generate an entire universe.

🛠️ How It Works:
	•	The universe isn’t pre-made.
	•	Instead, it generates planets, terrain, flora, fauna, weather, and more in real-time using procedural rules like:
	•	Noise functions (e.g., Perlin or Simplex Noise)
	•	Fractal iterations
	•	Mathematical symmetry and randomness seeds

One DVD, 80+ quadrillion planets 😮 — all based on math, not storage.

You said:
Thank you for the clear and detailed responses. With these clarifications, I can now proceed with implementing the MotherCORE project according to the specified requirements. Here's a quick summary of how these will be integrated:

### Implementation Plan Summary

1. **Plugin Configuration Persistence**
   - Store plugin states in a database table plugin_state with columns: name (primary key), enabled (boolean), and updated_at.
   - On startup, check the database for plugin states. If empty, initialize from the .env file using PLUGINS_ENABLED and persist to the database.
   - The PUT /plugins/{name}/enable|disable endpoint will update the database only. Changes will take effect on the next restart without needing file edits.

2. **Performance Baseline & Measurement**
   - Performance testing will be conducted on specified hardware: Apple M1/M2 or Intel i7 (4c/8t) or better, with 16 GB RAM, using Python 3.11.
   - The README will include a performance measurement method:
     - Warm-up with 5 discarded requests to /compute.
     - Measure the next 20 requests serially, ensuring p50 ≤ 500 ms and p95 ≤ 900 ms.
     - Log timings per plugin and total execution time in milliseconds.
     - Exclude initial database table creation from timing measurements.

3. **Error Handling & Validation**
   - Implement a uniform error schema for all non-2xx responses, including run_id, code, message, details, and optional hint.
   - Use the following status codes:
     - 400 for validation errors, including field errors in details.
     - 422 for malformed JSON.
     - 503 for database unavailability.
     - 500 for uncaught errors.
   - The health endpoint (GET /health) will always return 200 with a status of "ok" or "degraded," indicating database and plugin status.
   - For the CLI, use exit codes: 0 for success, 2 for validation errors, 3 for IO errors (file/DB), and 1 for other errors. Errors will print the same JSON schema to stderr.

4. **Deterministic Requirements**
   - Ensure serialization order of MotherResult keys using the Pydantic model for JSON output.
   - Maintain deterministic behavior with no randomness in plugins for v1. If randomness is introduced later, it will be seeded to ensure consistency.

With these directives, I'll proceed with the implementation and prepare the initial delivery as specified. If any issues or questions arise during development, I'll address them promptly. Once the initial version is ready, I'll deliver the zip file, directory tree, commit hash, test outputs, and an example curl request/response.

⸻

1) Plugin system & entry points
	•	Discovery: Discover plugins at startup (not hot-swap) via a registry (e.g., pluggy or a simple entrypoint loader).
	•	Contract (must implement):

class Plugin(Protocol):
    name: str                       # machine id, e.g. "gematria"
    version: str
    provides: list[str]             # fields it adds
    def configure(self, settings: dict) -> None: ...
    def compute(self, req: MotherRequest) -> PluginResult: ...


	•	Lifecycle: configure() once at boot → compute() per request.
	•	Enable/disable: Read from config (plugins.enabled=["gematria","numerology_gg33","lunar_13moon","resonance"]).
	•	Introspection API:
	•	GET /plugins → list name, version, enabled, provides
	•	PUT /plugins/{name}/enable|disable → toggle & persist to DB/config (affects next restart).
	•	No runtime unload/reload required in v1 (hot-reload can be a later feature).

⸻

2) Data handling & MotherResult shape
	•	Input (MotherRequest)

{
  "name": "Carlos Anthony Salazar",
  "dob": "1980-10-13",
  "birthplace": "Bellflower, CA",
  "current_city": "Norwalk, CA",
  "notes": null
}


	•	Output (MotherResult) — deterministic, structured:

{
  "run_id": "uuid",
  "requested_at": "ISO-8601",
  "completed_at": "ISO-8601",
  "engine_version": "semver",
  "request": {...MotherRequest...},
  "plugin_results": {
    "numerology_gg33": {
      "lifepath": 5,
      "personal_year": 9,
      "cycles": {...},
      "_meta": {"duration_ms":123}
    },
    "gematria": {
      "english": {"ordinal":  ... , "reduced": ... },
      "spanish": {"ordinal":  ... , "reduced": ... },
      "hebrew":  {"ordinal":  ... , "reduced": ... },
      "_meta": {"duration_ms":87}
    },
    "lunar_13moon": {
      "next_full_moon": "YYYY-MM-DD",
      "next_new_moon": "YYYY-MM-DD",
      "moon_age_days":  ...,
      "kin": "...",
      "_meta": {"duration_ms":64}
    }
  },
  "resonance": {
    "score": 0-100,
    "weights": {"numerology_gg33":0.4,"gematria":0.3,"lunar_13moon":0.3},
    "explain": [{"plugin":"...","contribution":...}]
  },
  "warnings": [],
  "trace": {"log_id":"..."}       // correlates to JSON logs
}


	•	Aggregation rule: run enabled plugins → merge their payloads under plugin_results → compute resonance using configured weights → return exact schema above.

⸻

3) Database swapping (SQLite ↔ Postgres)
	•	Goal: seamless switching via config only (no code changes).
	•	Config: .env → DATABASE_URL (SQLite by default), SQLMODEL_ECHO=false.
	•	Schema: create tables automatically on first run; include Alembic migrations but v1 can boot-strap with create_all() if migrations aren’t present.
	•	No special sync features required. Just be able to point at Postgres and run.
	•	Persist: store MotherRequest, MotherResult (JSON), plugin timings, and toggle states.

#### Frontend Setup

1. **Create React App**:
   
bash
   npx create-react-app my-app
   cd my-app
   yarn add antd @reduxjs/toolkit react-redux axios

javascript
   // src/store.js
   import { configureStore } from '@reduxjs/toolkit';
   import mySlice from './slices/mySlice';

   export const store = configureStore({
     reducer: {
       myFeature: mySlice.reducer,
     },
   });


3. **Create Slice**:
   
javascript
   // src/slices/mySlice.js
   import { createSlice, createAsyncThunk } from '@reduxjs/toolkit';
   import axios from 'axios';

   export const fetchData = createAsyncThunk('myFeature/fetchData', async () => {
     const response = await axios.get('/api/data');
     return response.data;
   });

   const mySlice = createSlice({
     name: 'myFeature',
     initialState: {},
     reducers: {},
     extraReducers: (builder) => {
       builder.addCase(fetchData.fulfilled, (state, action) => {
         // Handle data
       });
     },
   });

   export default mySlice;


#### Backend Setup

1. **Golang Server**:
   
go
   package main

   import (
       "github.com/gin-gonic/gin"
       "log"
   )

   func main() {
       r := gin.Default()
       r.GET("/api/data", func(c *gin.Context) {
           c.JSON(200, gin.H{"message": "Hello from Golang!"})
       })
       if err := r.Run(); err != nil {
           log.Fatal(err)
       }
   }


2. **JWT Authentication**:
   - Implement middleware to protect routes.

#### AI Integration

1. **Python Flask API**:
   
python
   from flask import Flask, request, jsonify
   from your_model import YourModel

   app = Flask(__name__)
   model = YourModel()

   @app.route('/api/predict', methods=['POST'])
   def predict():
       data = request.json
       result = model.predict(data)
       return jsonify(result)

   if __name__ == '__main__':
       app.run(port=5000)

2. **Implement JWT Authentication**:
   - Create middleware for protecting API routes.
   
go
   import (
       "github.com/dgrijalva/jwt-go"
       "net/http"
   )

   func AuthMiddleware() gin.HandlerFunc {
       return func(c *gin.Context) {
           tokenString := c.GetHeader("Authorization")
           token, err := jwt.Parse(tokenString, func(token *jwt.Token) (interface{}, error) {
               return []byte("your_secret_key"), nil
           })
           if err != nil || !token.Valid {
               c.JSON(http.StatusUnauthorized, gin.H{"error": "Unauthorized"})
               c.Abort()
               return
           }
           c.Next()
       }
   }


### 3.3 AI Model Integration

1. **Create Python Flask API**:
   
python
   from flask import Flask, request, jsonify
   from your_model import YourModel

   app = Flask(__name__)
   model = YourModel()

   @app.route('/api/predict', methods=['POST'])
   def predict():
       data = request.get_json()
       result = model.predict(data)
       return jsonify(result)

   if __name__ == '__main__':
       app.run(port=5000)

What’s Blocked
Why

You may use results to guide your own choices.
You cannot coerce or target others based on predictions.
Maintains karmic / ethical integrity.

Got it—your install is choking on hnswlib. That’s a super common Windows snag. Two ways forward:

⸻

A) Quick Fix (try this first — 2–3 minutes)
	1.	Make sure you’re on 64-bit Python 3.11:


python -c "import struct,sys;print(sys.version);print(8*struct.calcsize('P'))"

You want to see … 64.
	2.	Try pinned wheels that usually exist for Windows:

pip install --upgrade pip
pip install "hnswlib==0.8.0"

	3.	If those still fail, install via prebuilt wheel (works if a wheel is available for your exact Python):

============ ============ ============

You’re right to demand we design this so it can’t blind-side you again. I can’t promise the universe won’t throw a curveball, but I can give you a setup that (a) avoids the common Windows landmines, (b) fails softly (skips bad files, logs why), and (c) lets you make steady progress batch-by-batch.

Here’s the plan, with the failures I’m anticipating and the fixes already baked in.

Anticipated failures → built-in fixes
	1.	Wrong LLM / “needs OPENAI_API_KEY” error
• Cause: LlamaIndex defaults to OpenAI if you don’t bind a local LLM.
• Fix: In code we explicitly bind to Ollama (llama3.1:8b-instruct-q4_K_M) and to a local embedding model (sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2). No OpenAI key touched.
	2.	Rust / build-tool / hnswlib / Faiss headaches on Windows
• Cause: packages that compile C++ (or Rust) during pip install.
• Fix: Pin to Chroma versions that ship prebuilt wheels on Windows and do not require separate hnswlib installs. We won’t use Faiss.
• Also pin compatible versions to avoid dependency pinball.
	3.	Excel/HEIC/unknown mimetypes crash the reader
• Cause: unsupported file types in the folder.
• Fix: Only ingest .txt, .md, .json, .pdf. Everything else is skipped and written to logs/skip.log. (You can expand later.)
	4.	Big dump OOM / freezing
• Cause: trying to index 833k+ lines in one go.
• Fix: Incremental, resumable batching (default batch size 500 files). Persists after every batch. If it dies mid-run, rerun continues where it left off.
	5.	Path/permissions/AV interference
• Cause: long Windows paths, Bitdefender, UAC.
• Fix: short root path (D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE), create data, index, logs. Recommend adding D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE\.venv, index, and logs to Bitdefender exclusions.
	6.	PDFs that are scanned / encrypted
• Cause: pypdf can’t read some files.
• Fix: those get skipped and logged; later we can add OCR pass (Tesseract) if you want.
	7.	CUDA/Torch mismatch
• Cause: GPU not matching Torch build.
• Fix: Force CPU for embeddings to keep it stable now. You can flip to GPU later.

⸻

File 1: rag_ingest.py (robust, batched, resumable)

import os, json, time
from pathlib import Path
from tqdm import tqdm

from llama_index.core import VectorStoreIndex, StorageContext, ServiceContext, SimpleDirectoryReader, Settings
from llama_index.embeddings.huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbedding
from llama_index.llms.ollama import Ollama
from llama_index.core.node_parser import SentenceSplitter
from llama_index.core.storage.docstore import SimpleDocumentStore
from llama_index.core.storage.index_store import SimpleIndexStore
from llama_index.core.vector_stores import SimpleVectorStore

ROOT = Path(r"D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE")
DATA = ROOT / "data"
INDEX = ROOT / "index"
LOGS = ROOT / "logs"
INDEX.mkdir(exist_ok=True); LOGS.mkdir(exist_ok=True)

# ---- bind LOCAL llm + embeddings (no OpenAI!) ----
Settings.llm = Ollama(model="llama3.1:8b-instruct-q4_K_M", request_timeout=120.0)
Settings.embed_model = HuggingFaceEmbedding(model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2")
Settings.node_parser = SentenceSplitter(chunk_size=1024, chunk_overlap=100)

ALLOW_EXT = {".txt", ".md", ".json", ".pdf"}

def list_files(root: Path):
    for p in root.rglob("*"):
        if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in ALLOW_EXT:
            yield p
        elif p.is_file():
            with open(LOGS / "skip.log", "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write(f"SKIP (ext): {p}\n")

def load_docs(batch_paths):
    """Use LlamaIndex directory reader but only for allowed batch."""
    reader = SimpleDirectoryReader(input_dir=str(DATA), recursive=True, required_exts=list(ALLOW_EXT), filename_as_id=True)
    # LlamaIndex will load all allowed; we filter to our batch file names to control size
    idset = {str(p.resolve()) for p in batch_paths}
    docs = []
    for d in reader.load_data():
        if d.metadata.get("file_path"):
            if Path(d.metadata["file_path"]).resolve().as_posix() in {p.resolve().as_posix() for p in batch_paths}:
                docs.append(d)
    return docs

def persist_context():
    return StorageContext.from_defaults(
        persist_dir=str(INDEX),
        docstore=SimpleDocumentStore(),
        vector_store=SimpleVectorStore(),
        index_store=SimpleIndexStore(),
    )

def main():
    # resume state
    done_file = LOGS / "ingested.jsonl"
    done = set()
    if done_file.exists():
        with open(done_file, "r", encoding="utf-8") as f:
            for line in f:
                try:
                    done.add(json.loads(line)["path"])
                except:
                    pass

    all_files = [p for p in list_files(DATA)]
    todo = [p for p in all_files if str(p) not in done]

    if not todo:
        print("Nothing new to ingest.")
        return

    print(f"Found {len(todo)} files to ingest.")
    batch_size = 500  # tune up/down as needed
    storage_ctx = persist_context()

    for i in range(0, len(todo), batch_size):
        batch = todo[i:i+batch_size]
        print(f"\nBatch {i//batch_size + 1} / {((len(todo)-1)//batch_size)+1}  ({len(batch)} files)")
        try:
            docs = load_docs(batch)
            index = VectorStoreIndex.from_documents(docs, storage_context=storage_ctx, show_progress=True)
            storage_ctx.persist(persist_dir=str(INDEX))
            # mark done
            with open(done_file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                for p in batch:
                    f.write(json.dumps({"path": str(p)}) + "\n")
        except Exception as e:
            with open(LOGS / "errors.log", "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
                f.write(f"BATCH_FAIL {i}-{i+len(batch)}: {e}\n")
            # continue to next batch without dying
            continue
        time.sleep(0.2)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

from pathlib import Path
from llama_index.core import load_index_from_storage, StorageContext, Settings
from llama_index.llms.ollama import Ollama
from llama_index.embeddings.huggingface import HuggingFaceEmbedding

ROOT = Path(r"D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE")
INDEX = ROOT / "index"

Settings.llm = Ollama(model="llama3.1:8b-instruct-q4_K_M", request_timeout=120.0)
Settings.embed_model = HuggingFaceEmbedding(model_name="sentence-transformers/all-MiniLM-L6-v2")

storage_context = StorageContext.from_defaults(persist_dir=str(INDEX))
index = load_index_from_storage(storage_context)

query_engine = index.as_query_engine(similarity_top_k=5)
while True:
    q = input("\nAsk: ").strip()
    if not q: break
    print("\n--- Answer ---")
    print(query_engine.query(q))

(A) ingest_chroma.py — robust, batched, dedup, never dies

import os, sys, time, hashlib, json, traceback
from datetime import datetime
from pathlib import Path
from typing import List, Tuple

from pypdf import PdfReader
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer
import chromadb
from chromadb.config import Settings

ROOT = Path(r"D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE")
RAW_DIR = ROOT / "ingest_raw"
INDEX_DIR = ROOT / "index"
LOG_DIR = ROOT / "logs"
LOG_DIR.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

RUN_LOG = LOG_DIR / f"ingest_{datetime.now().strftime('%Y%m%d_%H%M%S')}.log"
ERR_LOG = LOG_DIR / "errors.log"

ALLOW_EXT = {".txt", ".md", ".log", ".json", ".pdf"}
CHUNK_CHARS = 1500
OVERLAP = 200
BATCH_SIZE_DOCS = 64
BATCH_SIZE_EMB = 64

def log(msg: str, file=RUN_LOG):
    with open(file, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(msg.rstrip() + "\n")
    print(msg)

def log_err(msg: str):
    with open(ERR_LOG, "a", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write(msg.rstrip() + "\n")
    print(msg)

def sha1_bytes(b: bytes) -> str:
    return hashlib.sha1(b).hexdigest()

def load_text(path: Path) -> str:
    ext = path.suffix.lower()
    if ext in {".txt", ".md", ".log"}:
        return path.read_text("utf-8", errors="ignore")
    if ext == ".json":
        try:
            obj = json.loads(path.read_text("utf-8", errors="ignore"))
            return json.dumps(obj, ensure_ascii=False, indent=2)
        except Exception:
            return path.read_text("utf-8", errors="ignore")
    if ext == ".pdf":
        try:
            reader = PdfReader(str(path))
            pages = []
            for p in reader.pages:
                try:
                    pages.append(p.extract_text() or "")
                except Exception as e:
                    log_err(f"[PDF_PAGE_FAIL] {path} :: {e}")
            return "\n\n".join(pages)
        except Exception as e:
            raise RuntimeError(f"PDF read failed: {e}")
    raise RuntimeError("Unsupported extension")

def chunk_text(text: str) -> List[str]:
    text = text.replace("\r\n", "\n")
    chunks = []
    i = 0
    n = len(text)
    while i < n:
        end = min(i + CHUNK_CHARS, n)
        chunks.append(text[i:end])
        i = end - OVERLAP
        if i < 0:
            i = 0
        if i >= n:
            break
    return [c for c in chunks if c.strip()]

def collect_files() -> List[Path]:
    if not RAW_DIR.exists():
        raise SystemExit(f"[FATAL] RAW_DIR missing: {RAW_DIR}")
    files = []
    for p in RAW_DIR.rglob("*"):
        if p.is_file() and p.suffix.lower() in ALLOW_EXT:
            files.append(p)
    return sorted(files, key=lambda p: p.stat().st_mtime)

def main():
    log(f"[START] ingest at {datetime.now().isoformat()} raw={RAW_DIR}")
    files = collect_files()
    log(f"[INFO] found {len(files)} candidate files")

    client = chromadb.PersistentClient(path=str(INDEX_DIR), settings=Settings(allow_reset=False))
    collection = client.get_or_create_collection(
        name="mothercore",
        metadata={"hnsw:space": "cosine"},
    )

    embedder = SentenceTransformer("all-MiniLM-L6-v2")  # small, fast, good
    added_docs = 0
    processed_files = 0

    for start in range(0, len(files), BATCH_SIZE_DOCS):
        batch = files[start:start+BATCH_SIZE_DOCS]
        ids, docs, metas = [], [], []
        for path in batch:
            processed_files += 1
            try:
                raw = path.read_bytes()
                file_hash = sha1_bytes(raw)
                # skip if already seen (dedup on file hash)
                # store as metadata "file_hash"
                # check existing by where filter:
                existing = collection.get(where={"file_hash": file_hash}, limit=1)
                if existing and existing.get("ids"):
                    log(f"[SKIP_DUP] {path}")
                    continue

                text = load_text(path)
                if not text.strip():
                    log(f"[SKIP_EMPTY] {path}")
                    continue
                parts = chunk_text(text)
                if not parts:
                    log(f"[SKIP_NOCHUNKS] {path}")
                    continue

                mtime = datetime.fromtimestamp(path.stat().st_mtime).isoformat()
                for idx, chunk in enumerate(parts):
                    ids.append(f"{file_hash}_{idx}")
                    docs.append(chunk)
                    metas.append({
                        "src": str(path),
                        "file_hash": file_hash,
                        "mtime": mtime,
                        "part": idx,
                        "total_parts": len(parts),
                    })
            except Exception as e:
                tb = traceback.format_exc(limit=1).strip()
                log_err(f"[READ_FAIL] {path} :: {e} :: {tb}")
                continue

        if not docs:
            continue

        # embed + upsert in mini-batches to avoid RAM spikes
        for i in range(0, len(docs), BATCH_SIZE_EMB):
            sub_ids = ids[i:i+BATCH_SIZE_EMB]
            sub_docs = docs[i:i+BATCH_SIZE_EMB]
            sub_metas = metas[i:i+BATCH_SIZE_EMB]
            try:
                embs = embedder.encode(sub_docs, batch_size=32, show_progress_bar=False, convert_to_numpy=True)
                collection.upsert(ids=sub_ids, documents=sub_docs, embeddings=embs.tolist(), metadatas=sub_metas)
                added_docs += len(sub_docs)
                log(f"[UPSERT] +{len(sub_docs)} chunks (total {added_docs})")
            except Exception as e:
                tb = traceback.format_exc(limit=1).strip()
                log_err(f"[UPSERT_FAIL] count={len(sub_docs)} :: {e} :: {tb}")

        log(f"[BATCH_DONE] files {start+1}-{start+len(batch)} / {len(files)}")

    log(f"[DONE] files seen={len(files)}, chunks added={added_docs}, errors logged to {ERR_LOG}")
    log(f"[INDEX_PATH] {INDEX_DIR}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

import chromadb
from chromadb.config import Settings
from pathlib import Path

INDEX_DIR = Path(r"D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE\index")

print(f"Documents (chunks) in index: {len(all_ids)}")

# Peek a couple metadatas
peek = col.get(include=["documents","metadatas"], limit=2)
for i,(doc,meta) in enumerate(zip(peek["documents"], peek["metadatas"])):
    print(f"\n--- SAMPLE {i+1} ---")

    print(doc[:400], "...")

(C) ask_local.py — retrieve from Chroma, answer with Ollama locally

import requests, json
from pathlib import Path
import chromadb
from chromadb.config import Settings
from sentence_transformers import SentenceTransformer

INDEX_DIR = Path(r"D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE\index")
OLLAMA_URL = "http://localhost:11434/api/generate"
MODEL = "llama3.1:8b-instruct-q4_K_M"  # or whatever you pulled

TOP_K = 5

def ollama_generate(prompt: str, model: str = MODEL, temperature: float = 0.2) -> str:
    data = {"model": model, "prompt": prompt, "stream": False, "options": {"temperature": temperature}}
    r = requests.post(OLLAMA_URL, json=data, timeout=600)
    r.raise_for_status()
    out = r.json()
    return out.get("response", "").strip()

def build_prompt(question: str, contexts):
    ctxs = "\n\n---\n\n".join([f"SOURCE: {c['src']}\n{c['text']}" for c in contexts])
    return f"""You are a concise RAG assistant.
Use the context below to answer the user's question. If unknown, say you don't know.

CONTEXT:
{ctxs}

QUESTION: {question}

Answer:"""

def main():
    client = chromadb.PersistentClient(path=str(INDEX_DIR), settings=Settings(allow_reset=False))
    col = client.get_or_create_collection("mothercore")
    embedder = SentenceTransformer("all-MiniLM-L6-v2")

    while True:
        q = input("\nAsk (or 'exit'): ").strip()
        if not q or q.lower() == "exit":
            break

        qvec = embedder.encode([q], convert_to_numpy=True)[0].tolist()
        res = col.query(query_embeddings=[qvec], n_results=TOP_K, include=["documents","metadatas"])
        docs = res["documents"][0]
        metas = res["metadatas"][0]

        ctx = [{"src": m.get("src"), "text": d} for d,m in zip(docs, metas)]
        prompt = build_prompt(q, ctx)

        try:
            ans = ollama_generate(prompt)
            print("\n=== ANSWER ===\n", ans)
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"[OLLAMA_FAIL] {e}")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()


⸻

(D) run_ingest.bat — one-click run + logging

@echo off
set ROOT=D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE
cd /d %ROOT%
call .\.venv\Scripts\activate
python .\scripts\ingest_chroma.py
echo.
echo Ingest complete. Logs in %ROOT%\logs\
pause



(Optional) ask_local.bat:

@echo off
set ROOT=D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE
cd /d %ROOT%
call .\.venv\Scripts\activate
python .\scripts\ask_local.py


⸻

3) Run order (no surprises)
	1.	Put files under D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE\ingest_raw\ (only: txt/md/log/json/pdf).
	2.	Activate venv:


cd D:\SYZYGY\MOTHERCORE
.\.venv\Scripts\activate


	3.	Start Ollama and verify it’s local:


ollama serve
# in another window:
curl http://localhost:11434/api/tags


You should see your local models listed. (If not, ollama run llama3.1:8b-instruct-q4_K_M once to pull.)

	4.	Ingest:

python .\scripts\ingest_chroma.py


	•	It logs to logs\ingest_YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS.log
	•	Any problem file goes to logs\errors.log and the run continues

	5.	Verify:

python .\scripts\verify_index.py

You’ll see total chunks and a couple samples.

	6.	Ask:

python .\scripts\ask_local.py

Ask something you know is inside your logs to prove it’s really local.

⸻

4) What we just “pre-fixed” (anticipated failure list)
	•	Excel / Word / images causing openpyxl/PIL/unstructured crashes → not used; unsupported types are skipped and logged.
	•	LlamaIndex auto-import surprises → not used at all.
	•	Memory blow-ups on huge dumps → batch 64 files → chunk + embed in mini-batches.
	•	Duplicates after re-runs → dedup by sha1(file); safe to re-run anytime.
	•	Corrupt PDFs / pages → try/except per page; error logged, run continues.
	•	Ollama not reachable → query script catches and prints [OLLAMA_FAIL] without killing session.
	•	Antivirus file locks → told you exactly what to exclude.
	•	Windows long paths / encoding → open with errors="ignore", use pathlib, avoid long path APIs.

I won’t promise “zero errors ever,” because that’s a lie—but I did build this so:
	•	a single bad file can’t take the pipeline down,
	•	you never lose progress, and
	•	you can see exactly what happened in logs/.

If anything still trips, paste the one line from errors.log and we’ll squash that specific case next.

🧩 THE PROBLEM YOU JUST DEFINED

Mission:

Extract key data from your PSA / SGC graded slabs automatically, organize them into your system, and give you flexible reporting & cool display outputs.

⸻

🔧 WHAT INFO WE NEED FROM EACH SLAB

At minimum, each graded card contains:

Field	Description
Grading Company	PSA / SGC / BGS / etc
Card Brand	e.g. Topps Chrome, Bowman, Donruss
Year	2023, 1997, etc
Player Name	e.g. Mike Trout
Card Number	Card serial number (e.g. #125)
Grade	e.g. PSA 9
Serial Number	Unique grading cert ID
Parallel / Insert Type	e.g. Refractor, Gold, 1/1, Autograph
Subgrades (optional)	If BGS / CGC style subs are present
Comments / Notes	Auto, Rookie, etc


⸻

🔬 3 OPTIONS TO GET THE DATA INTO SYSTEM

🅰️ Manual Entry (Baseline Start)
	•	You snap clear photos of each slab front (and back if needed).
	•	I assist you with batch data entry.
	•	Advantage: 100% accurate; good for initial seed database.
	•	Downside: Time-consuming if you have hundreds.

⸻

🅱️ OCR Extraction (Semi-Auto Middle Layer)
	•	Use Optical Character Recognition (OCR) tools:
	•	Google Vision AI
	•	Microsoft Azure OCR
	•	Tesseract (free + open source)
	•	You take clean, steady photos — OCR extracts the text off slabs.
	•	I assist with parsing and cleaning the data.
	•	Advantage: Much faster than manual.
	•	Downside: OCR may struggle with glare or poor lighting.

⸻

🅲️ API Scrape (Pro-Grade Long-Term Solution)
	•	Use the serial # off the PSA / SGC slabs to pull official data directly from:
	•	PSA API
	•	SGC API (or scrape from site)
	•	SportsCardPro database (excellent source)
	•	130point.com price lookup tools
	•	This auto-fills fields directly from the certification authority.
	•	Advantage: Near perfect accuracy + extra market data.
	•	Downside: Requires setup of scripts + API keys.

⸻

🖥️ WHAT HAPPENS AFTER DATA IS COLLECTED

Once we have the slab data, we can:
	•	✅ Display gorgeous dashboards with all your slabs.
	•	✅ Filter by player, grade, parallel, set, year.
	•	✅ Build reports on market trends.
	•	✅ Track your inventory value over time.
	•	✅ Build personal collection showcases for show-and-tell.
	•	✅ Simulate trades or sales.

### Comprehensive Project Documentation for SYZYGY ENTERPRISE Mobile Application

---

#### 1. **Project Overview**
   - **Project Title:** SYZYGY ENTERPRISE Addiction Support Application
   - **Objective:** The primary goal of this application is to provide individuals confronting addiction with essential resources, community engagement, and effective tools aimed at facilitating their recovery journey.
   - **Target Demographic:** This app is specifically designed for individuals seeking assistance and support in overcoming addiction challenges.

---

#### 2. **Requirements Specification**
   - **Functional Requirements:**
     - The application must allow users to create accounts and authenticate their identities securely.
     - Users should be able to track their progress through features like journals and various metrics.
     - The platform should include community forums and chat functionalities to foster peer support.
     - A comprehensive library of educational content on addiction and recovery strategies must be accessible.

   - **Non-Functional Requirements:**
     - **Performance:** The application should be designed to respond to user interactions within a two-second timeframe to ensure a seamless experience.
     - **Security:** It is crucial that all user data is encrypted and stored securely to protect sensitive information.
     - **Scalability:** The back-end architecture should be robust enough to accommodate an increasing number of users without compromising performance.

---

#### 3. **Technology Stack**
   - **Front-End Development Framework:** The application will utilize React Native for its ability to support cross-platform development, enabling a single codebase to function on both iOS and Android devices.
   - **Back-End Development Language:** The back end will be developed using Python, leveraging frameworks such as Flask or Django for efficient API management.
   - **Database Management System:** PostgreSQL or MongoDB will be employed to manage the application's data storage needs effectively.
   - **Cloud Hosting Platform:** The application will be hosted on cloud services like AWS or Azure, ensuring reliability and scalability.

---

#### 4. **Database Design**
   - **User Table Structure:**
   
sql
   CREATE TABLE users (
       user_id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
       username VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
       hashed_password VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
       email VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL UNIQUE,
       role VARCHAR(50) DEFAULT 'user',
       created_at TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP
   );


   - **Access Control Table Structure:**
   
sql
   CREATE TABLE user_access (
       access_id SERIAL PRIMARY KEY,
       user_id INT NOT NULL,
       feature VARCHAR(255) NOT NULL,
       access_level VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
       FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES users(user_id)
   );


---

#### 5. **API Development**
   - **Setting Up the Flask Framework:**
   
python
   from flask import Flask, request, jsonify
   from flask_sqlalchemy import SQLAlchemy
   from werkzeug.security import generate_password_hash, check_password_hash

   app = Flask(__name__)
   app.config['SQLALCHEMY_DATABASE_URI'] = 'postgresql://username:password@localhost/dbname'
   db = SQLAlchemy(app)


   - **User Registration Endpoint Implementation:**
   
python
   @app.route('/register', methods=['POST'])
   def register():
       data = request.get_json()
       new_user = User(
           username=data['username'],
           hashed_password=generate_password_hash(data['password']),
           email=data['email']
       )
       db.session.add(new_user)
       db.session.commit()
       return jsonify({"message": "User registered successfully!"}), 201


   - **User Login Authentication Endpoint:**
   
python
   @app.route('/login', methods=['POST'])
   def login():
       data = request.get_json()
       user = User.query.filter_by(username=data['username']).first()
       if user and check_password_hash(user.hashed_password, data['password']):
           return jsonify({"message": "Logged in successfully!"}), 200
       return jsonify({"message": "Invalid credentials!"}), 401


---

#### 6. **Front-End Development**
   - **Sample React Native Component for Registration:**
   
javascript
   import React, { useState } from 'react';
   import { View, TextInput, Button, Alert } from 'react-native';

   const Register = () => {
       const [username, setUsername] = useState('');
       const [password, setPassword] = useState('');

       const handleRegister = () => {
           fetch('http://yourapi/register', {
               method: 'POST',
               headers: {
                   'Content-Type': 'application/json',
               },
               body: JSON.stringify({ username, password }),
           })
           .then(response => response.json())
           .then(data => {
               Alert.alert(data.message);
           })
           .catch(error => {
               console.error('Error:', error);
           });
       };

       return (
           <View>
               <TextInput placeholder="Username" onChangeText={setUsername} />
               <TextInput placeholder="Password" secureTextEntry onChangeText={setPassword} />
               <Button title="Register" onPress={handleRegister} />
           </View>
       );
   };

   export default Register;


---

#### 7. **Testing Procedures**
   - **Unit Testing Example Using PyTest for Flask:**
   
python
   def test_user_registration(client):
       response = client.post('/register', json={'username': 'testuser', 'password': 'testpass'})
       assert response.status_code == 201
       assert b'User registered successfully!' in response.data


   - **Integration Testing Example for React Native Components:**
   
javascript
   import React from 'react';
   import { render, fireEvent } from '@testing-library/react-native';
   import Register from '../Register';

   test('successfully registers a user', async () => {
       const { getByPlaceholderText, getByText } = render(<Register />);
       fireEvent.changeText(getByPlaceholderText('Username'), 'testuser');
       fireEvent.changeText(getByPlaceholderText('Password'), 'testpass');
       fireEvent.press(getByText('Register'));
       // Add assertions for expected outcomes
   });

   - **User Feedback Mechanism:**
